ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-11-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00109_d7e4003	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Mr State Secretary! The social system in Austria is one of the main pillars of the Second Republic for social peace and progress in our country. We have recently taken note of the poverty report of the Republic of Austria, where we have seen that, due to the successes of this Federal Government, we are in the top of the European 15 and beyond. We have seen, ladies and gentlemen, that despite too high unemployment, unemployment among disabled people has fallen significantly from over 40 000 in 1999 under the socialist government to just under 30 000. It turns out, therefore, that the efforts made by the Federal Government in recent years have particularly affected the most socially in need in this country in a particularly positive way. The fact that the employment rate of women with disabilities has also improved significantly – in 1999, under Mrs Prammer, 38 per cent more employment, today by 10 per cent – is quite clear that the number of people with disabilities and all these measures in this area have been successful in recent years. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! There is also a constant discussion in Austria, in which social and social quotas are called into question. I can show you here a short statistics on the development of the social rate in Austria. [[the speaker holds a graphic in height]] The social rate in Austria has been significantly increased from 28.4 percent in 2000 to 28.7% in 2001 and 29.1% in 2002. Ladies and gentlemen! If one looks at the share of investment for families in the overall budget, it is clear from this curve that under the Social Democratic Government the share of investment for families was 6.5 percent, 1999 also 6.5 percent, and with the government Schüssel I and II increased to 8.3 percent in 2004 and 8.5 percent in 2005. [[the speaker shows another graphic]] I believe that one can make it clear with these curves that not only has there been a family-friendly social policy in Austria been mentioned under this federal government, but there has actually been a clear upturn of 6.5 percent that you, ladies and gentlemen of Social Democracy, have been responsible for today at 8.5 percent. – This is a beautiful success of this federal government in the social field for families. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! If you look at the differences in income between men and women – the income of men is shown here with 100 – you can also see that this federal government was successful: [[the speaker shows a graphic the other day]] In 1990, under socialist chancellorship, we had 71.2 percent, 2000, at the end of the Social Democratic governments, 81.2 percent, and in 2002 we had 82.2 percent and in 2004 almost 83 percent. Ladies and gentlemen! So you see, here too, the Federal Government has been successfully involved in the social upturn in recent years in the interest of women in this country. We have decided in recent years a number of social measures, and it is therefore of interest, Mrs Federal Minister, how and in what form the social system in Austria will be secured in future. With the pension reform and the harmonisation of pensions, we have redesigned the pension system for older generations, but also for young people in this country. I can add that if there are now again debates about the introduction of unemployment support for entrepreneurs, for farmers, the current 17.5% contribution benefits in the pension harmonisation system are also due to the fact that there is no insurance for unemployed entrepreneurs. So if you make a change in this area, the contribution rates will also be re-examined, because it will not be a question of taking the status quo on the one hand among workers and farmers, and, on the other hand, of disregarding changes in the fields of self-employment, of how these rates have been drawn up at the time. Therefore, I think it is interesting how these ideas will be implemented in the future and whether the corresponding contribution rates will be adequately increased to 22.8 percent if the public hand is to a decisive extent in the case of an unemployment insurance scheme for self-employed persons. Both, ladies and gentlemen, should not go, according to my own understanding: to continue to achieve lower contributions and to receive additional benefits from the public sector, which other professional groups receive. I therefore believe that there are a number of questions to discuss here, issues that are important for long-term security in Austria and which we also have to discuss in good time and comprehensively. Ladies and gentlemen! For the first time, we have been able to raise the care allowance again, and I also think it is important to point out here that the care allowance represents a crucial part of social security in Austria. Ladies and gentlemen! In the context of the care allowance, however, it should not be forgotten that the idea of an additive, i.e. an additional care check, is a long-term pursued idea not only by the Free Minister of Social Affairs, but also by parts of disabled organisations. And the introduction at that time of a general care contribution in health insurance in 2002 is also an important sign that the Austrian Parliament is largely opposed to such an additive form of care insurance in the then legislative period. I therefore believe that the excitement of the last 24 hours should be seen against the background of the facts. An improvement in these areas should be sought, an improvement in these areas is desirable. One should not have a discussion with When and But, but rather hold that in discussion, which was always the stock of the considerations of the Ministry of Social Affairs and the Minister of Social Affairs, namely to seek an additive solution with the care check, rather than to present the current services as being always at risk in the public debate. Ladies and gentlemen, I am convinced that, as a result of the demographic development in Austria, we will need such a development. In this area, we have achieved, for the first time, with the Article 15a agreements we have reached with the federal states, that the care and accompanying professions are recognised beyond the federal borders and therefore that young people also have new career opportunities and prospects. I do not think it is the right way to tolerate illegal employees in Austria in the long term, but it is in the long term the only right way to create employment in Austria through legality and through training Austrians in nursing and accompanying professions. Just as we, together with the Federal Ministry of Economic Affairs and Labour, managed to convert more than 2,500 people who were unemployed into care professions, it must succeed to meet the needs of Austria, namely, 10 000 caregivers, from their own areas by 2010. I don't like to tolerate illegality. But I think it is all to convert unemployed people into new, better-dominated occupations and to give them work and employment, and to make older people take care of people who understand them who have the same cultural background. It is about the elderly being cared for by people who are also close to them because of their cultural nature, and not by persons who also have language barriers to overcome and therefore only partially or not understand those who have been sent to care for them. We have a lot to do in this area, and it is therefore important for me to discuss this area comprehensively and forward-lookingly. – I wish the welfare state of Austria a good development.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-11-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00109_d7e5684	Mr. Federal Chancellor! Mr. Vice-Chancellor! Mrs. Foreign Minister! Mr President. We now advise and adopt the new European Constitution in the House with broad approval. This is an important one, like some of my historical step for Europe, for the political future of our continent, for the social future of our continent, for the economic future of our continent and for the social perspective of the citizens – but not only of the continent of Europe, but also of our country, our home country Austria. Ladies and gentlemen! In these days Austria also commemorates the history of Europe. 60 years ago, the regime of atrocities of the Nazi dictatorship and the Second World War had ended. But at the time, many people in Europe, including our homeland, asked themselves the question: How will it continue? What's the matter with you? the perspective for us and for our children after these horrors of World War II? On this question, ladies and gentlemen, there has been a clear answer from the responsible politicians of the continent, the countries of Europe. You said: The answer to the war in the sense of “Never again war!” lies in the strategy of the united Europe, of the united Europe! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] The answer therefore means: Europe when we talk about peace and want a peaceful development of our continent. ==References== May, just a few days ago, the day of Europe was celebrated. ==References====External links== May outline the perspective of the united Europe in a big speech, and we can be proud that an Austrian has also stood by the cradle of this thought. The Austrian Coudenhove-Calergi developed the perspective of the united Europe as a vision at the beginning of the last century. But with the united Europe, ladies and gentlemen, the European Union, then EC, not all questions were answered. After the Second World War, a separation, a border, a grave passed through Europe. Not all people were lucky to be able to live in the free united Europe. No, part of our continent is under the dictatorship of Communism, people have suffered. Also on this question, ladies and gentlemen: How do we get to the united Europe, how do we overcome the separation?, there is and there was a clear answer, and it also said: Europe. It is gratifying – and at times I do not think that this is at all appropriate to the dimension of the subject with which Europe is being discussed – that in our responsibility we have the past 1. May of the year 2004 where Europe has been able to overcome borders, so that today a truly united Europe is the perspective, ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Europe was thus the answer to the question of people after crossing borders. But what is the question of citizens, who are often asked to us, especially in a small country? – What is the challenge of the competitive situation that global globalisation poses? Yes, we must not shut our eyes to the fact that globalisation leads to new competitive situations. But also the answer to this question, to this legitimate question of citizens, is clear and clear. The answer to the fears of globalisation is: Europe. Why? – Because we are only able to compete with the giants of the world through the continence of the continent as a small country. Consider, for example, the development in China or India or in the United States. The answer to the fears of the people in relation to globalisation is: Europe. Europe is therefore also the perspective that we ourselves must pursue offensively and must live offensively. That does not mean, however, that we can speak out in Europe when it comes to the country's own position. The competitive position of Austria also depends on our own ability and on our will to make positive changes, to implement reforms that we have a strong Austrian position in the concert of strong Europe – and that is our goal, ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Herbs.]] But the people, the citizens, are also concerned, and one should also address them, such as when it comes to security. Especially in recent years, there have been developments in Europe that concern people, such as the question of organised crime and drug crime, the question of the cross-country dimension. Let's open this up. Here too, the question of citizens is right: How can we create more security? – The answer, ladies and gentlemen, is quite clear: Europe. We are no longer able to deal with everything ourselves when it comes to organised crime, we need European cooperation on security issues. But, of course, it also needs to be seen in terms of the prospects of a medium-term-oriented asylum policy in Europe. Europe also needs it in everyday life – think of consumer protection, food safety, animal welfare – I could list a lot now, up to environmental policy. Austria and Europe need this perspective of agreement. We need a strong Europe! Ladies and gentlemen, one thing is quite clear: A strong Europe needs a constitution! In the long term, and in the long run, we can only deal with the challenges that exist in the world – I have already said it – if you think of the USA, China, India – if we strengthen Europe's agreement. And the new European Constitution is one of the key contributions that Europe can actually fulfil the task that citizens expect from Europe. That is why we say about the Austrian People's Party out of firm conviction, out of responsibility for Austria and as a glowing European, yes to the European Constitution, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] But we must not be content with this, but we must also take criticism of the citizens seriously here and ask ourselves the question: Is this new European Constitution also the answer to the existing criticism of people? It is criticised, in my opinion quite rightly, that Europe is there or there too civil. It is precisely the new European Constitution that gives answers. In the future, 95 percent of the legislative acts will be decided with the European Parliament and the European Parliament. There is the European Citizens' Initiative, and there is of course also in the new Constitution – very interesting! – the eligibility of individual rights from fundamental rights at the European Court of Justice. The European Constitution is closer to the citizen and brings Europe closer to the citizens. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] A second criticism is that Europe is impenetrable – and the European Constitution is also the right answer: The institutions are clearer, we have a European foreign minister, so that Europe speaks with one voice, and we have also regulated the institutions very clearly and transparently with regard to decision-making, the European Council and the Commission. Europe is being criticized for being too centralistic. To this end, the new Constitution says: The principle of subsidiarity is the first time ever enshrined in this Constitution. What does that mean? – Europe should only do what Europe can, and the regions and the Member States should be given responsibility for what can be done better there. And I think that is right, ladies and gentlemen: a federalist Europe. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] It is criticised that, for example, the involvement of national parliaments has so far been too low. Yes, the new constitution gives us more opportunities to participate, here in the National Council, for example, through the subsidiarity action. I think it is right that this perspective is given, a perspective that treats the big and the small states alike. There is no difference between large and small in the European Constitution, but a scale: the same right for all! It is strengthening the social dimension. And it is also important that the new fundamental rights become directly effective, ladies and gentlemen. The social clause, called for by Austria, affects all political dimensions, including the role of social partners. And we have maintained – I thank both the members of the Convention and the Federal Government – that Austrian concerns, such as the safeguarding of water, the provision of services in the communities, are well regulated for Austrian citizens, ladies and gentlemen. On the criticism that is appropriate, for example, that neutrality would be abolished. I can only say that is simple and simply wrong. That the State Treaty is abolished is simply and simply wrong. In the case of critics of the European Constitution, I often feel that they do not criticise the Constitution, but actually mean something quite different, but then they should say what they mean. We want Europe, we want a strong Europe, ladies and gentlemen, with this new constitution! Finally, I would like to bring you a quote from Alois Mock, who wrote: A new era begins with the implementation of the Constitution for Europe. It strengthens our ability to act inside and outside. The regulatory framework defines much more clearly than previously the competences of the regions, the states and the Union. It will make Europe more accessible to its citizens, more capable of decision-making as a political Union, and more indisputable as a voice in the people's concert. - Quote. That is nothing to add, ladies and gentlemen, this is our Europe we want! We therefore agree with our responsibility of this Constitution and, of course, we also have the prospect of creating European rules on the game for European referendums on European issues in the future. That unites us, and that's our goal!

ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-11-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00109_d7e5887	Mr President. Federal Chancellor! Members of the Federal Government! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! If you see the pictures of the Second World War and the end of the Second World War, in view of the commemorations that have taken place in the last few days and will still take place, you can of course ask yourself the question: What happened in these 60 years? And: What's different? I agree with Mr Molterer: The European Union was the answer to the Second World War, and fortunately it is a very successful answer. [[We welcome SPÖ and ÖVP as well as representatives of the Greens and the Liberals.]] It is a successful answer because it has led to peace and security in that part of Europe, which belongs to the European Union or has previously been part of the EC. We have experienced over the past 15 years that in parts of Europe, which were not part of the European Union, there has been no peace and no security, but rather war, persecution, genocide and incredible human rights violations. If one compares the Europe of security with stability and that of Europe, which has also been shaken in recent decades, such as the countries in the Balkans, then I believe that the most successful peace project ever in the history of our continent is the European agreement and the European Union. We can be proud of this, and we can be glad that we have this European agreement. [[We welcome SPÖ and ÖVP as well as representatives of the Greens and the Liberals.]] Ladies and gentlemen! Many will wonder why, despite these undisputed successes of the European Union, as well as the full enlargement and the introduction of the euro, there is such a sustainable scepticism about Europe. If the European Union has been so successful, why are more and more people increasingly skeptical, especially in Austria? – I think we as the responsible person have to deal with the causes of this scepticism. I believe that one of the main reasons is that people in Austria, Mr Molterer, do not feel that Europe is the answer to globalisation, but that many people have the impression that the effects of globalisation in Europe will be intensified. This impression insecures many people because many have had the hope that Europe is an instrument for making globalisation more humane and social. But their experience is different. The question must be asked whether everything that is formulated at the level of absolute truths also by the EU Commission, particularly concerning deregulations and liberalisations, does not sometimes lead to people in Europe becoming more insecure in terms of the project as a whole, especially when the hopes of success do not arise. I don't think we should be surprised that people, even if we say: The euro is a very essential instrument for European economic policy!, with the right impression that the employment issue does not really go on, that income does not really rise, but rather sink there and there, and that then there are contexts made, given that many have the impression that Europe is moving away from its own goals and also from the promises there and there. I believe that this increasing scepticism has nothing to do with too much Europe, but actually with too little Europe, because Europe has not yet been able to fulfill those promises that many people have associated with this project. [[Consistency with the SPÖ and the Abg. May. Lunacek.]] By the way, I believe that this also applies to European democracy. Because what's the main problem? – If you are satisfied with a government in Austria, you can confirm it at the next election. If one is dissatisfied with one government, one can elect one government at the next election and replace it with another. The European problem, however, is that people do not know how, if they are dissatisfied with European policy, they will get rid of a European government that does not exist and replace it with another. They can participate in the elections to the European Parliament – this is important, but everyone knows it is not decisive about a European government. The European institutional system – there are good reasons why it is so – divides responsibility so widely that the normal interplay of democracy at European level is not possible. I believe that if one wants to see a stronger identification of the individual and the individual with the European project at European level, then we must eventually come to a European democracy that works in such a way as at national level that the individual citizen has the opportunity to elect or confirm a government. [[Abg. Scheibner: But it can always only be Parliament!]] [[Intrusion of the Abg. May. Molterer.]] I know that this is not a simple matter, but if you want to take up this dissatisfaction, you have to get there. We must ask ourselves the following question: If a person in Austria is not in agreement with or dissatisfied with politics or criticises the government, he will still not come to the idea of questioning Austria. If, on the other hand, there is criticism of Europe, there is criticism of European policy and if people are dissatisfied with European policy, then it is strange that Europe and the European Union are always called into question. And if you do not want to have that, because you think the European Union is important and indispensable, you must also create at European level a mechanism by which people can express their satisfaction or dissatisfaction with their policies. [[Consistency with the SPÖ and the Abg. Sburny.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I therefore believe that this EU Constitution represents an important step forward, but many of Europe's basic problems are not answered by the European Constitution. Of course, there are many who say that the European Constitution could have been better. I am of this opinion, too. I also believe that the proposal that the Convention has drawn up at European level was better than the draft before us today. Only: The alternative we have to decide on today is not this Constitution or a better constitution, but the alternative that lies ahead of us: either this Constitution or the continuation of the so-called Treaty Europe with an even greater uncertainty. Therefore, I believe, not the question between the good and the better, but the question arises between what you can get now and what else you would have. And if I am put before this alternative, I must say that this European Constitution is all better than the so-called “Contract Europe” so we should agree to this European Constitution together today. [[We welcome SPÖ and ÖVP as well as representatives of the Greens and the Liberals.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! There is also the discussion about why this Constitution is not a Christian democratic, not a social democratic, not a liberal, not a green constitution. This is the debate which is being held very strongly in France. I believe that the Austrian federal constitution is also unconstitutional. And a European Constitution cannot meet a particular political current in Europe, but must provide a framework for political shaping. That is why we must not arouse the illusion that the decision of this Constitution will already have better policies in Europe. But the EU Constitution provides the opportunity and presupposes that there will be better policies in Europe in the future. That is why this Constitution is not a guarantee but a chance. The last question you have to ask yourself is probably the: How does Europe look, should the Constitution not be adopted? Let us imagine the situation in Europe: Europe would be entering one of its most difficult phases and likely crises. I believe that in view of the challenges facing us and before Europe today, we should simply not afford to get Europe into a crisis, but we should try to make Europe more effective. Of course, it would be much better if all the people of Europe could have participated in a vote across Europe in order to carry this European Constitution together. I think it was a serious mistake that one cannot agree on such a procedure in the European Union, because that would have been the strongest signal – following the model that an Austrian constitution must have the support of the Austrian people, but that a European Constitution should have the support of all European peoples. That is why, I think, a great opportunity has been lost, because many of these processes, which exist today in individual countries of Europe, have little to do with the European Constitution and more about the individual state of the population in relation to the just rulers. To be honest, it may well be that in one country or another there is a referendum against the Constitution, although people do not actually have such a problem with the constitution, but perhaps with their government. The problem is in the end only: The Constitution is gone, but the government that people do not want remains. That's why I'd say: We in Austria vote on what we have to vote on: on governments and parliaments in elections and on the European Constitution in Parliament today! The Social Democracy will say yes.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-11-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00109_d7e6116	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! The Greens have long dealt with the advantages and shortcomings of this draft as a European Constitution. And we have come to the conclusion that a thorough consideration of the advantages that are undoubtedly present and some shortcomings which are also contained in the text or what is not contained in the text appears to be a clear yes to this Constitutional Treaty, for the following reasons which I would like to briefly outline. By the way: This Treaty on a Constitution for Europe is quite a black one. It covers printed, I think, almost 500 pages, the actual text part maybe 300. In comparison, the Austrian Constitution seems slender, even more elegant. But we also have 85 years of tradition, the EU not yet. So, from my point of view, what are the main benefits of this Constitution? First: In fact, European democracy is clearly strengthened in its foundations. This is evidenced by the fact that the only European institution directly elected, the European Parliament, is clearly strengthened in its right as a legislator and is much more involved in legislation than before. This was an essential criticism of the Greens in 1995 – but not just the Greens – that the Union's democratic approach was left more than to be desired and that it was really rudimentary at the time. At the same time, of course, violence separation in the European Union will be significantly improved as a basis for a functioning democracy. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Secondly, and this is also important in a Europe that currently has 25 Member States and is about to be 27 or 30: The Union's capacity to act will be significantly improved. This shows, for example, that the principle of unanimity between all Member States in order to reach a decision is clearly reduced in favour of a decision by so-called qualified majority. This is a double majority of the Member States on the one hand and a majority of the European population on the other, represented by them. This alone is a progress towards the Treaty of Nice. I believe that there were clear voting weighting rules in the European Council, which did not convince anyone. You can always talk about details, but the principle of the double majority is transparent, understandable and comprehensible. Thirdly, and this is an essential point: Part II of this new European Constitution, the Charter of Fundamental Rights, is a truly sensational success once you imagine how few other states in the world, nation-states, simple states or any state alliance have incorporated such a Charter of Fundamental Rights into their constitution. And this Charter of Fundamental Rights not only concerns classical liberal freedom and fundamental rights, such as freedom of expression, freedom of the media, freedom of the art, freedom of science and so on, freedom rights which, in some way, are intended to protect citizens from the state, but also shows substantial progress in the field of social fundamental rights. [[It is a pleasure to the Greens as well as to representatives of the SPÖ and the ÖVP.]] This is also important in so far as it is criticised by various sides that this Constitution has a neoliberal touchpage and the like. I can't understand. In the Austrian Constitution, for example, the fundamental social rights are not as enshrined in the European Constitution. That alone speaks against the argument of the neoliberal impact. There are, of course, points which are not to our satisfaction, and this applies to every political party and every citizen and every citizen of that country who is seriously dealing with them. From our point of view, some formulations in the field of military and security policy are particularly sensitive in Part III of the Constitutional Treaty or in the ecological field, despite some very well formulated targets, the unfortunate question of the Euratom Treaty. Now it can be said that it has already been a huge success that the Euratom Treaty will not, as originally planned, become part of the Constitution – that would have made it very difficult for us to agree to the European Constitution, if it had been possible at all – but will at least be located outside the Treaty, so that it is at least theoretically possible to be a member of the European Union without joining the Euratom Treaty. So, in total, I mean that the foundations for European democracy are actually laid here in an comprehensible, concrete way. ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-11-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00109_d7e6282	High house! Madam President. I should like to thank the Bureau and the Presidential Conference for the fact that the vote on this so important European constitutional work has been taken at a very important time, namely: ==References== April 2005 it was 60 years ago that the Second Republic was founded, and next Sunday it will be celebrated that 50 years ago the Austrian State Treaty was signed – and now we are putting into this very time the decision on this European Constitution. This has a deep sense: The State Treaty has freed us from the occupation, and this European Constitution frees us from being involved in a social, peaceful and economically strong Europe. And this is an important message of the present day! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Europe has not been built in one day, neither has Rome. The European Union was founded in Rome, and this Treaty has also been signed in Rome. It is, therefore, Roman treaties, which describe a process for many decades. We are not at the end of this process, but we are at a preliminary peak, and I believe that, as the previous speakers have stressed, we can say: From Victor Hugo to Coudenhove-Kalergi, to the famous Churchill speech and then the establishment of the European Economic Community, European history has been a history of success, peace and stability for 60 years. And it is very positive that we can also participate in this story. This Constitutional Treaty has not been invented now, but it goes back to December 2001, when we decided in Laeken Giscard D’Estaing as President of the Convention and set up a convention of 105 personalities who then prepared for almost two years of this constitutional work. ==References== ==References====External links==* Official website The Constitutional Treaty was completed in July of the following year 2003. As early as October 2003, we founded the Intergovernmental Conference in Rome. At the time, Benita Ferrero-Waldner was on the road with me, who I would like to thank here for her work. Finally, we did on 29 October. October 2004 may sign this Constitutional Treaty: the Foreign Minister Ursula Plassnik and myself. We have been granted the authorisation in two stages with a Federal Constitutional Act, unanimously – at this point thanks to the National Council, to all four political groups here in the House – and today we are adopting this Constitutional Treaty. All those who think that more information should have been given have obviously overlooked the fact that we made public events before the beginning of the Convention, especially with the youth. We debated publicly for hours before the start of the Intergovernmental Conference. We held several discussions here in the House all day, before and after each European Council, had public meetings of the main European committee and then discussed this Treaty several times. And I say, I am also proud of this work! Mr Van der Bellen! Ursula Plassnik and I have It smiles very neutrally – as do the 1.2 million households. It is not supposed to be a propaganda brochure, but a pure information about what is in it in the 200 pages of the real contract; we can ignore the attached protocols quietly. In my opinion, it is important that this Treaty really sets the foundations for the citizens of Europe: Every European citizen has a passport, not only an Austrian passport, but also a European passport. With this new constitution, every citizen is given European citizens' rights. It can, for example, encourage its rights in any European country. He can choose. He has the opportunity to use consular assistance in third countries, which can be a very important point especially in the case of a flood wave in Southeast Asia. He's part of the Charter of Fundamental Rights, he can sue them. It can pursue the Union and its institutions up to the European Court of Justice and it can warn that its law will be implemented. I really think this is an essential step that will actually bring us into an open, civil and democratic Europe, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] We have the opportunity for the first time to launch European citizens' initiatives. Think of topics that can be significant, such as animal welfare, which we have also brought into the Constitution as a target catalogue – a subject that has moved us very much in Austria. There is now the possibility of forcing the institutions of the Union to deal with such issues by means of a European citizens' initiative. Think of transport policy, environmental issues, food safety and many other issues that are pressing on there! Or remember that in the event of procedural defects, discrimination or abuse of the institutions of the European Ombudsman can be called on! Who says that this Constitution contains too little democracy, too few elements of democracy, should also remember that for the first time we are granting the European Parliament powerful rules of co-determination – something that is, of course, for us in Austria, but not yet in all European countries. Almost 100 per cent – I believe 95 or 96 per cent – of all European laws are in the future only if the European Parliament is involved in the legislation. Ministers must, when they meet in councils and pass laws, publicly meet in this case. There is also the possibility of subjecting the European Council to the control of the European Court of Justice. National parliaments are being strengthened. You will be given the opportunity to determine whether you have any right to complain or to pull the brake if, for example, in questions of subsidiarity, the small units are threatened in any way. So I think that these are precisely the elements to be shown for those critics of European reality who say: That's still not perfect enough! – Yes, I think so, but at the same time say: This means that there are elements in this Constitution that have never been valid so far! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] We Austrians, like any other Member State, have, of course, also mentioned some national issues which the public should know: the anchoring of minority protection, the explicit statement of the principle of equality between men and women, the anchoring of the principle that all Member States are the same, that, especially in the case of staff, such as the European Council Presidency, the Commission Presidency, the Foreign Minister, there should not be any specific features for large or certain regions, but that all really must be taken into account in terms of balance. Animal welfare, which I have already mentioned, animal welfare, price stability, border regions: Everything from our point of view important issues, which are also included in this Treaty. There are some critics – and I am grateful that Professor Van der Bellen pointed out – who say that the Treaty is neoliberal. If so, why does the European trade union movement support this Treaty unanimously and fully? It really sets out the most important principles for the Social Union, such as that of full employment in Article I-3 rather than the earlier principle of “high employment standards”. It is now no longer just a high level of social security, but full solidarity, the fight against abuse, the fight against discrimination, social justice as a fundamental principle. Another point that is being discussed over and over again in these days is the question of the priority of European law over national law. This question is dealt with in Article I-6 of the European Constitution. Hand at heart: How else should a European Community work? How else should a European economic area work if, for example, national rules on European Community rules are applied? The principle is not really new either, it has already been pointed out: For 40 years this has been true in the Judicature. At that time, an Italian, Flaminio Costa, sued his electricity bill for protesting against the nationalization of the Italian electricity company ENEL. The Luxembourg judges have given him the right – by the way, in the interests of consumer protection. To be honest, when we joined – 30 years after this knowledge – everyone knew that we are joining a European Union in which the priority of European law – but only in those areas in which there are Community competences – applies to national or national or municipal competences. That's good, too, and nothing has changed. The Austrian people also took note of this during the referendum eleven years ago, with an impressive two-thirds majority. I think it is a matter of course, ladies and gentlemen, that these things should be spoken out and stood up to them. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Allow me also to say something about referendum. . I have already been very surprised that honourable Member Voggenhuber has complained that the Federal Government, I personally or my representative and the government groups did not strongly support a Europe-wide referendum idea. I would just like to remind you, Professor Van der Bellen and honourable Members of the Green Group: There is a common document, a common document from my government representative, my personal representative Hannes Farnleitner of 31. March 2003, which, by the way, John Voggenhuber has signed, as well as 15 other Convention members – at least 15 percent of the entire Convention – in which exactly this idea, which I publicly supported in September 2003, before the start of the Intergovernmental Conference, is inside, namely, a European vote on the same day throughout Europe, in all 25 Member States, so that for the first time such a constitution, a European Constitution, is also approved by the European people. [[Keeping the same in height]] The government parties have supported it, the Green Group has also supported it – others would have been invited, but have not participated, but I expressly welcome the fact that the Social Democrats today are also in favour of this common idea. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] I then went to the European Council three more times later. ==References== ==References== November 2004, this idea propagated, but remained all alone. I'm saying it here, too. That's also happening, and I'm not ashamed of it at all. I think we're more here than other nations. I've always warned of a “flicker slug. ” Now nine countries are making their own referendums, and this is spread over almost two years. This has practically resulted in European work in important areas, because no human being now dares to propose various things in the Commission or in Parliament or in the councils, which may be controversial, because some national referendum, which is usually about very different issues, is under discussion. In Austria we have a broad support for this idea of a Europe-wide referendum. 52 percent of Austrian citizens are in favour of such a Europe-wide vote, which means implicitly that it rejects a national vote. Ladies and gentlemen, come to the conclusion: 40 years ago, on Monday this week, a great Austrian died, a great Austrian celebrates his 40. Date of death: Leopold Figl. I chose his farewell speech as Federal Chancellor and Party President from 1951, which contains a touching vision. Leopold Figl then said: The United States of Europe is the goal to be achieved after all historical ties have been overcome. The path is still set by numerous obstacles. And yet, it seems that this idea of a European agreement is increasingly dedressed from its utopian character by the harsh compulsion of history and is entering the stage of realization. This would create an order in Europe, with an economic potential that would allow the solution of the economic and social problems which could not be solved by the individual national economies so far. - Quote. And today, 2005, we are still not quite, but still, a huge bit closer to this dream. I therefore ask: Let us vote together for this important step, a peaceful one, a social one, an economically strong one and creating democratic Europe for its citizens – with a joint decision on the new Constitution!

Last Monday, on Europe's day, I was at an event where the question was raised: What is the geographical centre of Europe? No one knew that; understandable. The geographical centre of Europe is Riga. It can therefore be seen that Europe is much bigger and not just the European Union. 25 states have joined forces in this European Union. 400 million people are trying to go a common way, and this is a political vision that is of an immense force. It was only the traumatic experiences of the Second World War that created this strong political force and this long breath, so that a process as difficult as the unification of Europe and the further development of Europe became possible. What is special about the European Union? – This is especially the attempt to preserve diversity while at the same time pursuing common ways and finding common solutions. And it is precisely that very many criticisms are inflamed. Finding a constitution for Europe is not easy. Many different countries are involved, and each country has different ways and different ideas. And this student, born long after the World Wars, replied that the most important thing for her is still peace. I consider the opinions expressed by these professors to be relatively critical on their own because they have a completely different credibility. The fact that a political debate is taking place is something different than when professors are different about their own subject, namely a constitutional issue of different opinions, one day before the corresponding vote. And this is also possible with this new constitution. However, we also see – and this has to be said quite clearly – that, in all these continental problems, a country alone is too weak to meet these challenges, that we need European cooperation in order to achieve all these objectives in order to ensure economic, social and environmental good development for Europe. Ladies and gentlemen! The European Union and its mechanisms are also our answer to the negative effects of globalisation. ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-11-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00109_d7e6912	Madam President. Federal Chancellor! Ladies and gentlemen on the Government Bank! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) I would like to make a general comment, namely the question: What do you need a constitution for? What kind of meaning does a constitution have? – That’s something you don’t always know what that means, because it’s so natural. A Constitution restricts the arbitrariness of state action and also has the task of defining, by establishing fundamental rights, freedoms and rights of citizens vis-à-vis the state. And that's something very important. The opposite of a constitutional state is a dictatorship. – Creating a constitution at European level is therefore a very important concern. Historically, the European Union has come into being: with three Treaties, which are based on very different considerations, namely EURATOM, the favour of a single source of energy, the Treaty on the European Coal and Steel Community, and the European Economic Community. And from this economic burden, the so-called four freedoms in the internal market have increasingly developed very economically. The whole other formation, namely the fundamental rights, has failed, and the situation of the European Constitution has so far been that it was a Torso that only one side existed and the other has not. That is why it is so important that this European Constitutional Treaty now sets out for the first time a catalogue of fundamental rights, for the first time setting out the missing second link to these fundamental freedoms. [[As a result of the Commission's proposal, the Commission has decided to extend the scope of the common position to the Member States of the European Parliament and of the Council on the application of the principle of equal treatment for men and women in ……]] The Austrian Greens responded with criticism and skepticism when it came to the question of whether Austria should join the European Union. This vision has not been achieved. The Austrian population has opted for the European Union, then still the European Community, with a very, very large majority. We now see our task of working at all levels to improve the European Union: that it becomes more ecological, that it becomes more sustainable, that it becomes social, that it becomes a peace power in the world – who else is to do so worldwide? – and we work for it. For this purpose, Johannes Voggenhuber is working in the European Parliament and here we are also working in this Parliament. That is why our answer to this discussion is: EU Constitution: yes or no? a very clear one, namely: Yes, we want them, but we want to change them too! We want to further develop them, and we want what is now to be improved. If I hear these critical voices pretending to be an alternative now, as if you could just want the ideal constitution, I think it is a little shortsighted. You always have to fight for the better, and you can't say: I don't want that, but I don't want to see an alternative. At the moment, there is only one alternative to this Constitutional Treaty. And the task of all the critics is to work to ensure that this improves – but not to prevent it. It is a right to education – every person has the right to education and access to vocational training and further education – and it is even a positive discrimination against women – that would have been unthinkable in Austria, since there have been many votes against from your side –; there is a right to social participation, independent life, cultural participation for older people, for disabled people, for people with disabilities; and there is also such a thing as collective workers' rights, the right to strike, for example – this too is not yet anchored in our Austrian federal constitution and is not yet in sight. These are achievements at European level, given that everyone and everyone who opposes this Constitution must be aware of the fact that they are also arguing against a European catalogue of fundamental rights, which is a real milestone throughout the fundamental rights discussion and that the positive impetus is not only for European constitutional development but also for the development of the whole world towards peace union, sustainability, environmental and social security. All these are important points, as I mean. It is also important from my point of view that it is also clear how to deal with serious infringements of the Union's values by a Member State, because this new regulation would prevent something that Austrians did wrongfully with the arbitrary and non-contractual sanctions imposed on us in 2000 as I believe. The integration of the Charter of Fundamental Rights of the European Union into the Constitutional Treaty also makes classic civil and political rights binding, but also social rights and principles. These are all important things that a democrat can only assess positively. I also believe that the improvement of the legal protection of individuals within the EU in relation to Union acts and the verifiability of the European Council's actions is worth mentioning and very positive, also because the Austrian initiatives are, as I know, essential to the strengthening of the Union's legal system. Ladies and gentlemen! They know – I have mentioned it more often – that I am not only a federalist, but also a glowing friend of subsidiarity and I believe that they can be applied as often as possible. That is why I believe that it is also worth mentioning and very, very positive that it includes precisely compliance with subsidiarity, this principle, and also a described review in this Constitution, and that there are opportunities for national parliaments, such as you are one, as we are here, to object to proposals from the European Commission when a violation of this principle of subsidiarity is evident in it. I could mention the citizens' initiatives, which can be made possible – i.e. a means of direct democracy – I could also mention the social clause in which it is written that a high level of employment is written in order to ensure adequate social protection and to stand up for it, I could mention the solidarity clause and much more. However, I would like to mention something particularly positive, namely that it is also a matter of re-establishing a step towards greater security, more peace for Europe and Europe. And this, ladies and gentlemen, is not measurable at all, neither with numbers nor with money. This is not materially measurable, but it is a value that is not at all affordable and that we must always mention positively. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Finally, in the given short time on the subject of EURATOM. It has already been critically mentioned that there has been some failure, neither in the Convention nor in the Intergovernmental Conference. I believe that the long-term objective must continue to be the gradual expiry of the EURATOM Treaty. EURATOM funds may be used exclusively for the decommissioning of installations, and in the future an express legal basis for Europe-wide security standards must also be the objective and subject of initiatives of Austrian EU policies. This seems to me to be a point we must continue to follow. It's not as successful as we all imagine it, I think. But in total, Member States gain more opportunities to maintain sovereignty and ownership, more security – skills are defined, procedures are standardised, fundamental rights are declared – i.e. greater legal certainty, more democracy. Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that this Constitution can be accepted with good conscience. It is a constitution in the Europe of the regions. Of course, we must live this Europe of the regions themselves.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-11-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00109_d7e7686	Madam President. Federal Chancellor! Mr. Vice-Chancellor! High house! Ladies and gentlemen! Let me add a few comments from the point of view of the foreign policy on this new European constitutional work. We have no less than the first joint negotiation success of the enlarged European Union before us, the Union of the 25 and that is a novum in the history of Europe. 25 sovereign states have jointly established the rules for their cooperation. They have agreed on the values that will determine their actions, they have agreed on the objectives they wish to pursue together, and they have also laid down the rules of law which will determine the daily balance of interests. This Constitution thus creates a piece of modern European identity – and this in full respect for the cultural, economic and security characteristics of the Member States. This Constitution will be – that was yes already said – for 450 million people, but it will also create a very concrete bond of commonness: In the future, it connects each individual European with each other – whether Hungarian or Latvian, whether Portuguese or Austrian. This Constitution is not only blue with golden stars, it is also red-white-red; it also contains the colours of our partners in the European Union. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] I also find it remarkable that this first joint success of the 25 is just the foundation for our future action. The negotiation process itself was a positive experience for Europe, for there has never been such a democratic and transparent process before. We forge alliances with other partners, with partners who naturally had similar interests. However, we have not only worked together for negotiation goals, but also created a permanent network that is still positive today. The debate we are having today is important for Austria, but it is also important in other countries. Five of the European Member States have already ratified the Constitution: Lithuania, Hungary, Italy, Greece and Slovenia. In Spain, it is almost ratified after a referendum. Today, that day, Slovakia’s people’s representatives will discuss this Constitution and make their decision; tomorrow the Bundestag will do so in Berlin. So you see, ladies and gentlemen, we are in agreement, we are also joining the European Community on this point. And you, ladies and gentlemen of the House, have today's decision as a clear sign of a reunited Europe capable of action and forward-looking. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP as well as on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] In terms of foreign policy, the new Constitution not only sets out the instruments and functions that will strengthen foreign policy in the future but also the objectives of European Union action in the field of foreign policy. And I would like to quote briefly from Article III-292 (1): "The Union can be guided at international level by the principles relevant to its own development, development and enlargement, and which it also wants to strengthen in the world: Democracy, the rule of law, the universal validity and indivisibleness of human rights and fundamental freedoms, respect for human dignity, the principle of equality, the principle of solidarity and respect for the principles of the Charter of the United Nations and international law.’ – Quotes. Ladies and gentlemen! This Constitution will therefore also provide the basis for Europe's responsibility in the world to be met tomorrow. However, the Constitution will also strengthen the awareness that Europe together has more weight and enforcement than individual Member States. Today, this continent is safer, more free and rich than ever in our history. What moves people today is to safeguard the specific European model of life, a competitive social market economy, a society characterised by pluralism and tolerance, a high level of environmental protection, social justice and social protection, equality between women and men, solidarity and diversity. I particularly welcome the representatives of the young generation who have come here to the Austrian Parliament today; it is an important day for them: [[towards gallery]] It will also decide on its future; They will have to carry on and develop the European integration work. [[General condolences.]] I would now like to close by commenting on the motto of the European Union: In diversity. – This is the goal, which is also a fundamental principle that carries us. Europe does not arise in one day, and not through a treaty, but through very concrete acts, through a “solidity of the facts”, as this Robert Schuman described. You, ladies and gentlemen, are taking such a step today. It is a step towards confidence, a step of confidence in our common European future. Thank you.

It is noteworthy that, despite the fact that the European Constitutional Treaty has also worked with highly centralistically organized countries, the future EU constitution of local self-government gives a higher priority than the Austrian federal constitution. For the first time in the history of the EU, the Constitutional Treaty explicitly respects regional and local self-government. This has not always been easy to achieve, as cooperative federalism is further developed with the involvement of the communities in Austria than in most other European countries. I still remember very well the introduction of an amendment by the Heads of State of Tyrol and South Tyrol, which referred to the competence for water so important to us. After a few discussions in the Committee of the Regions, we have tabled our amendment: Water remains in Austrian hands. This is particularly important for the countries in which a lot of water flows. The principle of unanimity is only available in a few areas, but also in terms of water. [[President Dipl.-Ing. Prinzhorn shall take over the chairmanship.]] The Constitutional Treaty also makes it clear that European laws do not affect public health, such as health, transport and waste disposal, and that decisions remain the responsibility of each Member State. The border and mountain regions are particularly taken into account in the new Constitution, which is of great importance to Tyrol, but especially to East Tyrol. In the future, there will be a structural programme for border and mountain regions. Austria has prevailed in many areas, so I would like to thank the negotiators in particular, especially our Federal Chancellor Wolfgang Schüssel. Thank you.

Based on the expectation that Europe could become stronger, that Europe could be a stronger political factor and that Europe would also have to have greater economic importance in the world economy, I believe that the agreement to it in the other countries will be found in whatever way. This is important because it will depend on how we can position ourselves in the future. I believe it is important that, for example, the qualified majority is laid down in this Treaty, so that it will be impossible for small states to be overwhelmed by the larger ones. The dual majority-finding ensures that we too, a small country in the large Europe, can manifest our importance. Mr. Federal Chancellor, that was certainly a great achievement. As so much criticism has already been made today, these positive things must also be made very clear. I think it's important that we talk about it. Furthermore, I am glad that this Treaty will ensure that social rights are indicted accordingly and that, in accordance with the horizontal social clause, we may also be able to expect a new development. For me, important elements are peacekeeping, the preservation of this valuable good and the joint action against any kind of new threat, such as crime and terror. My expectation is that, before the next growth step, Europe will live and practice this Constitution, which we will decide today in Austria.
ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-11-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00109_d7e9272	The next speaker was Mrs Hlavac. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-11-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00109_d7e9295	Mr President. Federal Chancellor! Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The European Union was expanded from 15 to 25 members a year ago. This was a big, important project, but the institutions have to be adapted to it. For this reason, but also for many other reasons, it was necessary to develop a new constitution. A fall back on the Treaty of Nice certainly cannot be the solution. Therefore, there will be almost unanimous agreement today, even if criticism has been expressed, and I think that criticism of the European Union is also justified, because expectations have undoubtedly not been met. Remarkable is the mini-referendum of the Austrian Society for European Policy, in which 5 000 people have been questioned. 37 percent thought that today the National Council should give its approval to the EU Constitution, 21 percent were against it, and 42 percent did not express their opinion. It is very interesting that almost half of the respondents did not express any opinion. That already indicates a lack of information. This information deficit is simple. Of course, the Constitution is a very complicated work; people look at less treaty texts than the effects on their daily lives. That is where we find distrust, but also disappointment with the European Union. I do not think so much rejection as distrust because the European Union is not seen as the Europe of people. People associate bureaucracy, inconsistency, abjectness. However, everyone must also be aware that the policy of the European Union is still the sum of the government's policies and therefore also the governments bear the responsibility, play a key role in criticism of the EU and disappointment over the European Union. It is therefore important that the Constitution provides a framework for policy and that targets are articulated, some of which are quite positive. Thus – and this is very important for us Social Democrats – the social issues of higher importance are given. The objectives include full employment, combating social exclusion and discrimination, promoting social justice and social protection, equality between women and men, solidarity between generations, protection of the rights of the child and also an anchoring of social society. It is also important to include the Charter of Fundamental Rights in the Constitution. However, there are also negative things to mention. There are no macroeconomic differences in growth and employment. There is no way to stop down-to-down tax competition, and there is no clear guarantee of public services or general provision of services in the context of EU competition law. That is to say, there is still an overweighting of the economic over the social situation. Nevertheless, as has been said, it is important for the European Union to be further developed. It is important that it develops into a social union, and that requires a good constitution. This Constitution is certainly just a first step, but after all, it is taking a first step.

For example, we discuss publicly in the main committee before each European Council, as the Federal Government also has Austrian interests to represent. We have a subcommittee of the main committee, which deals with detail issues and there we discuss with the competent member of the Federal Government, how we Austrians should behave at European level, but we only have the opportunity in plenary to talk about European issues when it comes to such a subject as the European Constitution, that is, if a treaty is to be voted on by yes or no. I think that it is right that we should be more concerned about future issues of the European Union in the future in the National Council. That is the content of this amendment concerning the Rules of Procedure. I think it is very positive to see that in future, in the National Council, we will discuss in our own meetings what a work programme of a Presidency looks like. We can discuss all these future issues that are related to this. And that will also give due attention, more than before. Secondly, at a meeting of the National Council, we will be able to discuss issues proposed by each of the clubs, with a Rules of Procedure similar to those in the case of a urgent question. We will be able to deal with such future issues from the institutions' work programmes, and a club can propose this. We also have the opportunity to discuss matters discussed in the main committee in public in plenary, so to speak. I think these opportunities are a good development, because I expect that when the national Parliament deals with them, a subject will be much more important in the field of education and training. It is the subject of the European future. This is a development after ten years of membership of the European Union, which I believe we all welcome. All the political groups voted in favour of this in committee. I think this is also a major political development, because we will only be able to raise the public awareness of European issues if we are prepared in the National Council to devote more attention to these future issues of Europe in a public debate. That is why I see this as a very good step. I am pleased with the agreement of all the political groups and believe that this first step, even if not everything is still perfect and we probably need to change some of the rules of procedure from the point of view, is a very successful step. It is not a real progress if one wants to participate in EU matters, but it does not make it possible in essential new points. Let us also look at all the possibilities that exist for normal parliamentary operations for EU meetings, and consider further possibilities: Networking with other parliaments for these meetings, opportunities for EU bodies to speak here, to answer, to question them! Let us be a little more courageous on this question! Then the meetings become more exciting, the transfers are more exciting – it is better for us, for the public, and it is better for the European idea too.

In concrete terms, Europol is currently responsible for illicit drug trafficking, trafficking in human beings, car crime, terrorism and illegal trafficking in nuclear and radioactive substances, and today it is intended to be extended to prevent and combat money laundering. Another and essential point – and this has also been addressed by my previous speaker – is the re-regulation of the data query. The data survey has so far been allowed only to a limited circle of Europol officials. The national authorities will now also be able to raise a general question. Content surveys may only be made by Europol as before. Europol must, at the same time, create an instrument for ensuring the legality of data surveys. This is, I believe, a principle in this amendment to the Europol Convention: The legality of the data surveys must be ensured! Of course, the issues of data protection law are constantly raised in the case of extension of competence in the police sector. As I said, The Austrian The Data Protection Council reviewed the amendment of the Europol Convention and found it to be in order. Let me add another comment: It is not easy to guarantee the greatest possible security on the one hand and at the same time guarantee absolute freedom. If security is to be ensured for citizens, then the legal framework must also be agreed. Today, we are taking an important forward-looking step towards combating international crime. Austria is thus committed to a Europe with greater security. We stand for a modern Europe, which we have already discussed in depth today, which is also able to protect its citizens as best as possible.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-11-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00109_d7e11083	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Europol aims to improve the performance of the competent authorities of the Member States and their cooperation in order to increase the fight against international organised crime and to contribute to the prevention and fight against terrorism. In order for Europol to be able to play its central role effectively in European cooperation, it is necessary to make constant adjustments to meet the requirements effectively. In addition to improving cooperation between Member States, the current amendment to the Europol Convention, in particular the re-regulation of tasks in relations with third countries and third countries relating to euro counterfeiting, will be adopted. By extending the area of competence to prevent and combat money laundering, Europol has an effective tool to prevent and combat serious international crime. In order to be able to act in the best possible way, direct information exchange between Europol and the national authorities is necessary in compliance with the data protection directives and simplification of the processing systems of asylum analysis data. A logging of the queries of personal data from information collections is a necessary means of suffocating data abuse in the germ. That is why we are striving to do this. The current crime statistics, ladies and gentlemen, show that the crime rate in Austria fell by 4 per cent in April and the education rate increased by 1.7 per cent. This confirms Austria's modern and efficient way to combat crime and in turn testifies that Austria is one of the safest countries in the world. Mrs Federal Minister, continue along this path! We could thus put a volume of investments of about €6 billion into the grid, into the expropriation of new or old power plants. In the field of trade regulations, there is a significant increase in the simplified procedure to 800 square meters: Employment volumes of approximately 4,000 to 5 000 jobs per year. In addition, a broadband offensive is coming especially for rural areas, in which the accusation is sometimes raised that it will be diluted, the now planned doubling of the broadband offensive from € 10 million to € 20 million, and the invitation to the federal states – I hope they will join them – to make another 10 million loose for it. Then we would have €30 million for the development of rural areas in particular. Minister for the Environment Josef Pröll has ensured that such broadband networks are included in the funding conditions in the settlement and water management funds. Employment volume: 200 jobs. Then Ecostrom: Extension of the period for compulsory first-time feed from already approved installations by one and a half years until the end of December 2007. Direct direct employment effect: 3 000 jobs. Sixteenths: Invite the social partners to launch an offensive for more flexible, practical working hours. We very much hope that this will make it possible to break through the wage rounds in autumn. We believe that this can lead to a substantial cost-saving and an increase in competitiveness, which could well mean 2 to 5 percent improvement in competitiveness. The seventh point is a qualification offensive, first in language early promotion, in day-to-day care. Over 300 pedagogues will be able to find an additional job immediately from autumn. The cost is significant: We spend 120 million € alone for this apprenticeship promotion. In total, we expect about 2 000 additional permanent employment relationships. After all, more than 6 000 illegal jobs have been found and indicated last year. If half of us were legalized, we would have a great success, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] The tenth point is the continuation of the Internationalisation and Export Plan. This “go international” is a very sustainable impulse for the export economy. Today, goods and services account for just over 50 percent of gross domestic product. The offensive is on full tour. Last year we were the European champions in export growth. We would like to hold this position and hope that the continuation of the offensive in the economic sector will achieve a sustainable level effect. We hope that next year we will break the sound wall of 100 billion in exports. The additional employment effect could be about 200 jobs. Ladies and gentlemen! If one calculates this carefully, with the IHS and also inquires back on the banks' economists, we believe that in the short term 15 000 additional jobs, perhaps even 25 000 in the medium term, can be secured. Above all, the tax reduction will be added at the beginning of the year, which has brought 25 percent Köst-Satz a sustainable location impulse and, ultimately, with a billion-and-a-half-billion discharges in the broad consumer layers, also significantly stimulates consumption. In this context, it is important to me that: We will only get entrepreneurs and investors in Austria if we do not make the mistake now, for example, coinage or some others in Germany by calling entrepreneurs and investors locusts who come to eat and move a country empty. I say to you, ladies and gentlemen, I am grateful for every farm that is located here, which is investing here, creating jobs, paying taxes and thus contributing to our red and white-red success. And this should also be said here in the House. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] [[Abg. The 2000 consolidation pact only provided for a balanced government budget for 2002. – According to the report, the zero deficit was already achieved in 2001 with a surplus of 0.3 percent of the gross domestic product and thus a year earlier than planned or confirmed by a low deficit of 0.2 percent in the following year. The Court of Auditors report highlights that consolidation was mainly carried out in 2002 through expenditure-side measures. The expenditure ratio, i.e. public expenditure in relation to gross domestic product, fell from 54.2 per cent to 52.4 per cent of GDP. I come to the conclusion: In line with the objectives of the Federal Government, the Court of Auditors stresses the long-term stabilisation of public finances and refers to the need for a balanced general government budget, even though in the short term the budgetary deficit is expected to increase, in particular through tax reform 2004 and 2005, and this in the sense of the Austrian economy. Differences in income in this area are, after having a gender-neutral remuneration in the public sector, in order to explain and thus justify that women are less in leadership positions or are also more in part-time employment. One area, which, I believe, is also important to mention, concerns the average gross annual income of pensioners, who resided in Austria, which rose by 2.04 percent in 2002 compared with the previous year, or 2.44 percent in 2003, with the adjustment factor according to the ASVG according to § 108 para. 5 in 2002 was 1.011 percent and 1.005 percent in 2003. This substantial deviation to the adjustment factor and to the actual increases is due to the so-called cohort effect, i.e. that pensioners who have a lower income, de facto die away – and pensioners who go after, i.e. the new pension, have a higher pension than such. As the Court of Auditors is repeatedly asked questions or asked by Members within the Committee and in the framework of telephone calls, and it is considered necessary, I also intend to review the report in pages 5 to 11 and 27 to 50 and to include additional explanations in this area or to make the explanations more meaningful. This relates to the comparability of the incomes of women and men, of employees, public servants and pensioners, and I will try, in conjunction with Statistics Austria, to provide you with the statistical part, namely the statistical and technical Annex, in an electronic way. In this context, it should be mentioned very briefly that the Court of Auditors – I do not wish to do so – according to § 8 para. 1 to 3 was also unable to report on the benefits and pensions of persons who are entitled to them from legal entities subject to the control of the Court of Auditors. I can only expressly point out that you are required to create new rules in this regard, because, in this report and in the consequence, we are not in a position to comply with this report, since, on the basis of a decision of the Constitutional Court, we do not call for a name-named disclosure of the references or for surveys to be carried out on the ground in order to arrive at those data. Thank you.

Just once, what it was about: The Supreme Court ruled in a decision relating to the insolvency of the BHI bank in Graz: If the auditor is indebted in the proper review in the years preceding the bankruptcy, the Republic of Austria shall make an official liability for that examiner within the framework of its official liability. In other words, in this case, the Republic has to pay compensation for damage beyond the security of deposit. If you want to change this jurisdiction, that is something that is based on the provisions of Article 23 paragraph. 1 B-VG requires a two-thirds majority. This is also known by the Federal Government, for it itself has in its explanatory remarks on its own government proposal on Article 2 para. 5 expressly stated: ‘Since the scheme provides for a special provision on the type of aid.
