A number of negative trends are currently meeting. The increasing smuggling from the East, Eastern Europe and China. Secondly, we have increased tobacco tax in the course of the financial balance on the country's desire, and thirdly, we have the famous anti-smoker campaigns. All these influences are negative and result in the first quarter of the Trafiken having a 20 percent and more turnover reduction. It is therefore urgently necessary to increase the minimum level of trade today, which is also a requirement of the Trafikants. I would also like to say very clearly what is often overlooked: The trafiks have a multiple function! We have considered the labour market policy of 3,000 specialised businesses, which represent the basis of living for 10,000 people and their families. The Trafiken also have a regional policy function. We have a lot of close-up suppliers today who could not survive without additional income from the traffic. We have 5 000 so-called tobacco sales outlets to local suppliers. Thirdly, the Trafiques have a social policy function, ladies and gentlemen. 42 percent of all tobacco shops are run by disabled persons. The trafiks have a health policy function fourth. Not every business must sell tobacco, and with the increase in the trade range, we want to avoid cigarettes coming to the market very cheap for reasons of youth protection and health policy. Cheap cigarettes are not in the interests of youth protection or health policy! Finally, ladies and gentlemen, I say this to the conclusion that this can only be a first step for the trams. Further steps must follow. In particular, in the sense of our resolution, we must take energetic measures against cigarette smuggling, in particular to achieve coordination of border arms, the Federal Criminal Office and customs administration, and, secondly, we must give more room for action in the future in the field of by-products in order to ensure that they are fair and fair. There is a chance to be a basis of existence for tens of thousands of families in this country as well.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-11-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00109_d7e20995	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! From the Greens it will be – but I say that with regret! – can unfortunately not give an agreement to these two agreements. I am going to justify this now, because otherwise those who were not in committee wonder why this is so. In principle, it makes sense in the context of the expansion of political dialogue with the states of the Andean Pact and also the Central American States to establish a stronger link between the European Union and these States. The problem we have here is that these agreements are more or less just a continuation of agreements that already exist, for example between the EU and Mexico or between the USA and Latin America. The present agreements are primarily designed to prepare free trade agreements within the meaning of the first mentioned ones, but in a form where a post- sustainable development within the meaning of the three axes of ecology, economics and social affairs is not to be borne. That is one of the reasons for our rejection. It is not really understandable, for example, why experience resulting from studies commissioned by the European Commission itself in order to determine, for example, the effects of the EU-Mexico Agreement or the Free Trade Agreement between the United States and Latin America have not been included in the present Agreements. I am referring in particular to the negative experience of the destruction of local agriculture, for example, or the loss of 1.3 million jobs in agriculture in Mexico since 1994 in the case of the Agreement between the USA, Canada and Mexico, and the fact that it was not possible to create only nearly as many jobs in the Maquila industry, i.e. in the so-called export production zones. – This is an example. Furthermore, environmental destruction, the concentration of production in the hands of transnational companies, and the promises of political dialogue were not met either. All of these are analyses that emerge from a study commissioned by the European Commission itself and which was then presented. In this respect, it is incomprehensible that the present agreements now take more or less exactly the same measures that are criticised by previous agreements and simply rewritten. Another criticism is that the very different political, economic and social situation in the individual states is not at all happening. Many of the countries of Central America, for example Guatemala and El Salvador, have a civil war past, which has not yet been worked out, but others have not. The decades-long war in Colombia is also not taken into account. There is a single note that the reintegration of the illegal armed forces is to take place. But we know that this is primarily about the paramilitarys. In this respect, these agreements have a headline: Unfortunately, they do not address the living conditions of the majority of the population. That is why we cannot accept this agreement. Thirdly, I come to the area of migration: In this respect, the Agreements set out the declaration of will that illegal migrants should be withdrawn and that such readmission agreement should be concluded as soon as possible. Regional agreements are increasingly calling for these readmission agreements, just recently with Russia from the European Union. We know, however, that in practice this can often mean quite repressive measures. – That is the third reason why we cannot accept these agreements, so important we find the political dialogue with this region. But, unfortunately, we cannot agree in this case.

A hundred more women are affected each year, most of them going to Africa, more than 80 percent. In this area, information work is absolutely necessary. Vienna also promotes a consultancy service where medical and mental advice is offered. The funds, however, are not quite sufficient. This means that the Confederation is also called upon to make available funds. I expect the Federal Government to declare female genital mutilation as an asylum ground. This is not yet foreseen in the context of the revision of the Asylum Act – to the shame of many African migrants. - Thank you.

Point Report by the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection on the proposal from the Commission to the Council (Doc. Ulrike Lunacek, ladies and gentlemen, on swift action against massive human rights violations and crimes against humanity in the Darfur Provinces (Sudan) (940 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-11-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00109_d7e21829	We'll get to 37. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. The debate opens up to Mr Dipl.-Ing. May. Controller. His welcome time is 2 minutes. Please, my colleague.
ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-11-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00109_d7e21856	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! ==References== At the end of the 19th century, the European Powers of Colonies began to compete in the interior of Africa. England and France, in particular, were fiercely competing with each other at the time, leading to the famous Faschoda crisis. As their solution, the spheres of influence of the two powers, then also with the other countries, were laid down, and the result is that borders were created are not on tribal borders or on ethnic boundaries or on large landscapes. After decolonization, these borders remained such – no natural boundaries, but any border shift would have led to wars of unimaginable scale. Of course, there are now massive conflicts in these countries in Africa. Today we are dealing with Sudan, Africa's largest land state, where for many decades there has been a war between the Christian-Animist South and the Black African population and the Arab-Islamic population in the north, which finally led to a peace settlement in May 2004. This conflict, however, has also spread over the Darfur provinces, and in order to act on it, the Arab-Nomadic tribes were armed by the Sudanese government, and the reaction was of course that the Darfur tribes have resisted. The result is, in any case, murders, rapes, lootings, kidnappings, forced recruitments of children, and all of this often after bombardment of the Sudanese Air Force. Hundreds of thousands of people are already fleeing, tens of thousands have died. The humanitarian situation is disastrous, both in the Darfur provinces and in the refugees in Chad. Some things have already happened. The African Union is using peacekeeping forces but has far too little money for them. Aid organisations are trying to improve. However, they are not supported enough by the Government in Khartoum and do not have the appropriate protection. The UN Security Council has decided resolutions, and the UN Human Rights Commission has condemned human rights violations. In order for more to happen, we are deciding today on a resolution. We address our foreign minister and ask her to continue to support the peace process in Darfur and also to support the rapid implementation of UN Security Council resolutions. I hope that these measures will help to ensure that peace will soon be established in these unhappy Darfur provinces.
